## Verifying this bundle

This directory is self-checking. Independent checks, in the order a sceptical reader
would run them:

1. `sha256sum -c MANIFEST.sha256` — every file, the capture included, is covered.
2. The evidence verifier re-reads `capture/run-20260909-210225.pcapng` and confirms that every cited frame
   exists and carries the exact bytes each assertion claims. It reads only this
   directory and needs nothing from the bench that produced it.
3. Every case verdict in the table above is re-derived from that case's own printed
   assertions. A stored verdict may be stricter than they roll up to — an uncited PASS is
   downgraded on purpose — but never weaker, so a headline cannot drift away from, or be
   edited away from, the evidence underneath it.
4. The DUT metrics scrapes below are re-derived from the raw exposition bodies in
   `metrics/`: every value, delta and outcome must follow from the bytes shipped with
   them. That proves the readings were recorded faithfully — not that the device
   was telling the truth about itself.

Assertions marked *(no digest)* below carry no re-checkable citation: they are
narrative, not proof.

## Test cases

### ✓ csip-conf-v1.3::COMM-002 Basic Discovery (Out-of-Band) [C, A, S] — PASS

Reference: CSIP-CONF-v1.3 V1.3 §5 Communication Fundamentals Tests

Live-phase observation (what the check's own socket saw; the numbered assertions below are re-derived from the capture and are the verdict): out-of-band discovery: waited 3s for the DUT's poll cycle against the configured 2030.5 endpoint; poll-cycle window: 2m30s, derived from the bench 2030.5 server's advertised pollRate of 1m0s (poll_rate_s from http://192.168.0.188:11114/admin/status), which is the rate a client in poll_rate_mode "honor" — the product default — paces its walk at, the configured interval being only a floor beneath it — it is the slower of that and the DUT's own IEEE 2030.5 discovery interval floor of 1m0s (discovery_interval_s in /etc/lexa/northbound.json), and the slower governs: 2 periods plus 30s of slack, so a period that had just elapsed still leaves a whole one inside the window; VERDICT RECONCILED — the live phase declared SKIP from what the socket saw; the citation phase re-derived the criteria from the capture and the case is PASS. The capture-derived verdict is the one that stands: it is the only one a reader of this bundle can repeat.

Frame attribution: 145 frame(s) (91–4040), precision endpoint; 92 frame(s) inside the window belonged to other conversations and were excluded.

Connections claimed: tcp *:* <> 192.168.0.188:11113 (the DUT dials OUT to the bench's 2030.5 server, so this check cannot know the DUT's ephemeral local port and cannot claim a full 4-tuple. The claim is therefore every TCP frame to or from the CSIP server endpoint during this check's interval. That is sound on this bench because the endpoint is the gridsim simulator and the gateway under test is its only client; it would NOT be sound if a second conformance run were driving the same simulator concurrently, which is why live runs in this campaign are serialized).

1. **PASS** — the DUT reached the 2030.5 server at the out-of-band configured address and port
   - Method: cleartext TLS handshake in the capture — the TCP conversation attributed to this test case, whose remote endpoint is the configured server address
   - Observed: TLS session 192.168.0.69:41406 <> 192.168.0.188:11113 established to the configured endpoint 192.168.0.188:11113
   - Frames: 99
   - Frames sha256: `eb57463b3e57060ef1dfd9c502095fc42ea5f4562287e13c64605e1d112a869b`
   - Note: frames attributed by remote endpoint and time, not by connection 4-tuple: the DUT dials OUT to the bench's 2030.5 server, so this check cannot know the DUT's ephemeral local port and cannot claim a full 4-tuple. The claim is therefore every TCP frame to or from the CSIP server endpoint during this check's interval. That is sound on this bench because the endpoint is the gridsim simulator and the gateway under test is its only client; it would NOT be sound if a second conformance run were driving the same simulator concurrently, which is why live runs in this campaign are serialized
2. **PASS** — the DUT used the out-of-band configuration rather than service discovery: the capture contains no mDNS/xmDNS or SSDP query from the DUT
   - Method: cleartext TLS handshake in the capture — a scan of the WHOLE run capture for UDP traffic on the service-discovery ports, since an absence cannot be attributed to a single test case's frames — counted per SOURCE ADDRESS, because only datagrams the DUT itself sent bear on this claim
   - Observed: the DUT (192.168.0.69) sent no mDNS/xmDNS or SSDP datagram; the capture holds 2273 from other hosts on the bench segment (192.168.0.1×153 192.168.0.102×74 192.168.0.124×423 192.168.0.141×284 192.168.0.144×11 192.168.0.169×1 192.168.0.188×2 192.168.0.198×1 192.168.0.220×166 192.168.0.243×328 2601:18a:827e:752f:4429:18d2:55e2:3562×2 fe80::14d4:578f:42bb:3a32×78 fe80::1867:5529:5158:9897×122 fe80::8f9c:8d6c:db2:6f84×328 fe80::c0c:1420:5cd5:cb9b×8 fe80::c2f:1eff:fe63:4f00×292), which do not bear on this claim
   - _(no digest — narrative, not re-checkable)_
   - Note: not wire-cited; source: the run's capture, but the evaluator did not record which frames carried the fact
3. **PASS** — the DUT issued GET /dcap and the server answered 200 with a conformant DeviceCapability
   - Method: decrypted HTTP/2030.5 transcript from the capture (NSS key log) — the request line, status line and sep+xml body of the first /dcap exchange in the session
   - Observed: GET /dcap -> 200 <DeviceCapability pollRate=60> TimeLink×1 ResponseSetListLink×1 EndDeviceListLink×1 MirrorUsagePointListLink×1 SelfDeviceLink×1; root element in the 2030.5 namespace with an EndDeviceListLink
   - Frames: 123, 126
   - Stream: `192.168.0.188:11113 > 192.168.0.69:41406` bytes [1283,1841)
   - Bytes sha256: `e9d014a27e35463f39cbe47dd01ee79be6477b8fb05a057c3ec5f495e7b9a74e`

Live-phase observation (what the check's own socket saw; the numbered assertions below are re-derived from the capture and are the verdict): certificate chain length 3 (SERCA -> MICA -> device certificate); poll-cycle window: 2m30s, derived from the bench 2030.5 server's advertised pollRate of 1m0s (poll_rate_s from http://192.168.0.188:11114/admin/status), which is the rate a client in poll_rate_mode "honor" — the product default — paces its walk at, the configured interval being only a floor beneath it — it is the slower of that and the DUT's own IEEE 2030.5 discovery interval floor of 1m0s (discovery_interval_s in /etc/lexa/northbound.json), and the slower governs: 2 periods plus 30s of slack, so a period that had just elapsed still leaves a whole one inside the window; VERDICT RECONCILED — the live phase declared SKIP from what the socket saw; the citation phase re-derived the criteria from the capture and the case is PASS. The capture-derived verdict is the one that stands: it is the only one a reader of this bundle can repeat.

Frame attribution: 182 frame(s) (15040–16035), precision endpoint; 826 frame(s) inside the window belonged to other conversations and were excluded.

Connections claimed: tcp *:* <> 192.168.0.188:11113 (the DUT dials OUT to the bench's 2030.5 server, so this check cannot know the DUT's ephemeral local port and cannot claim a full 4-tuple.    - Observed: the bench is already serving a swapped chain ("COMM-004 invalid-mica-eku-critical", leaf 949247e5bfc6b345…) rather than its start-up chain ("startup", leaf 6dd91ff098cabd8f…) — another run left a fixture installed, and this sub-test will not stack a second one on top of it; the bench is already serving a swapped chain ("COMM-004 invalid-mica-eku-critical", leaf 949247e5bfc6b345…) rather than its start-up chain ("startup", leaf 6dd91ff098cabd8f…) — another run left a fixture installed, and this sub-test will not stack a second one on top of it
   - _(no digest — narrative, not re-checkable)_
   - Note: not asserted in this run
2. **SKIP** — the bench's 2030.5 server was returned to its original certificate chain after this sub-test, so no later test case runs against a poisoned bench
   - Method: not observed — gridsim's chain lever was told to restore, and the chain it serves was then READ BACK and compared to the start-up chain by leaf SHA-256 — the POST returning 200 and the bench actually serving the original chain being two different facts
   - Observed: no chain was installed, so there was nothing to restore
   - _(no digest — narrative, not re-checkable)_

### ✓ csip-conf-v1.3::CORE-010 Function Set Assignments — PASS

Reference: CSIP-CONF-v1.3 V1.3 §CORE-010 - Function Set Assignments [C, A, S] (pp. 43-45)

Live-phase observation (what the check's own socket saw; the numbered assertions below are re-derived from the capture and are the verdict): function-set-assignment walk to the DERProgramList; the procedure's seven-program topology fixture is not what this bench serves — see the DERProgramList assertion; poll-cycle window: 2m30s, derived from the bench 2030.5 server's advertised pollRate of 1m0s (poll_rate_s from http://192.168.0.188:11114/admin/status), which is the rate a client in poll_rate_mode "honor" — the product default — paces its walk at, the configured interval being only a floor beneath it — it is the slower of that and the DUT's own IEEE 2030.5 discovery interval floor of 1m0s (discovery_interval_s in /etc/lexa/northbound.json), and the slower governs: 2 periods plus 30s of slack, so a period that had just elapsed still leaves a whole one inside the window; VERDICT RECONCILED — the live phase declared SKIP from what the socket saw; the citation phase re-derived the criteria from the capture and the case is PASS. The capture-derived verdict is the one that stands: it is the only one a reader of this bundle can repeat.

Reference: CSIP-CONF-v1.3 V1.3 §7 Basic Functions Tests - BASIC-004 - Basic Inverter Control (Low/High Voltage Ride-Through) [C, A, S]

Live-phase observation (what the check's own socket saw; the numbered assertions below are re-derived from the capture and are the verdict): published ONE DERControl (CERT-BASIC-004-ed61bab1) carrying opModLVRTMustTrip + opModLVRTMomentaryCessation + opModHVRTMustTrip + opModHVRTMomentaryCessation and waited 1m3s for the DUT to fetch it; the independent southbound oracle says: the DER did NOT hold this row's content before it was published (this row published <opModLVRTMustTrip> 7 breakpoint(s) (150, 0) (150, 5000) (1200, 5000) (1200, 7000) (2200, 7000) (2200, 8800) (10000, 8800) (x multiplier 10^-2, y multiplier 10^-2) = (1.5 s, 0) (1.5 s, 50) (12 s, 50) (12 s, 70) (22 s, 70) (22 s, 88) (100 s, 88) in the device's own units, yRefType=4 (%setEffectiveV) northbound and the DER's M707 DER Trip LV MustTrip sub-curve adopt handshake never COMPLETED, so no curve-set was taken up: M707 DER Trip LV live curve-set MustTrip — Ena=0 (disabled), adopt req=0 rslt=0 (not COMPLETED), NPt=8 NCrvSet=2 (each set holds MustTrip/MayTrip/MomCess), curve-set read-only=true, MustTrip s/%VNom points: (2, 50) (21, 88) <- this reading, MayTrip holds NO points, MomCess holds NO points. IEEE 2030.5's low-voltage ride-through curves are carried by SunSpec model 707 (DERTripLV). One curve-SET holds THREE sub-curves — MustTrip, MayTrip, MomCess — each with its own ActPt and its own point table, which is why this row's arms name a sub-curve and not just a model. Each point is Pt.V (uint16, units "VNomPct" — per cent of NOMINAL voltage, scaled by V_SF) against Pt.Tms (uint32, units "Secs", scaled by Tms_SF). THE POINT NAMED Tms IS IN SECONDS: model_707.json says "Secs", and a referee that read the name as milliseconds would be wrong by a factor of 1000 in the direction that certifies — a 0.16 s trip commanded and 160 s held would compare equal. This referee decodes the pair as (seconds, %VNom), which is the order IEEE 2030.5's own ride-through DERCurve uses; the REGISTERS store V first AND this row published <opModLVRTMomentaryCessation> 2 breakpoint(s) (0, 6000) (150, 6000) (x multiplier 10^-2, y multiplier 10^-2) = (0 s, 60) (1.5 s, 60) in the device's own units, yRefType=4 (%setEffectiveV) northbound and the DER's M707 DER Trip LV MomCess sub-curve adopt handshake never COMPLETED, so no curve-set was taken up: M707 DER Trip LV live curve-set MomCess — Ena=0 (disabled), adopt req=0 rslt=0 (not COMPLETED), NPt=8 NCrvSet=2 (each set holds MustTrip/MayTrip/MomCess), curve-set read-only=true, MustTrip s/%VNom points: (2, 50) (21, 88), MayTrip holds NO points, MomCess holds NO points <- this reading. THE POINT NAMED Tms IS IN SECONDS: model_707.json says "Secs", and a referee that read the name as milliseconds would be wrong by a factor of 1000 in the direction that certifies — a 0.16 s trip commanded and 160 s held would compare equal. This referee decodes the pair as (seconds, %VNom), which is the order IEEE 2030.5's own ride-through DERCurve uses; the REGISTERS store V first AND this row published <opModHVRTMustTrip> 5 breakpoint(s) (16, 12000) (16, 11000) (1200, 11000) (1200, 10000) (10000, 10000) (x multiplier 10^-2, y multiplier 10^-2) = (0.16 s, 120) (0.16 s, 110) (12 s, 110) (12 s, 100) (100 s, 100) in the device's own units, yRefType=4 (%setEffectiveV) northbound and the DER's M708 DER Trip HV MustTrip sub-curve adopt handshake never COMPLETED, so no curve-set was taken up: M708 DER Trip HV live curve-set MustTrip — Ena=0 (disabled), adopt req=0 rslt=0 (not COMPLETED), NPt=8 NCrvSet=2 (each set holds MustTrip/MayTrip/MomCess), curve-set read-only=true, MustTrip s/%VNom points: (13, 110) (0.16, 120) <- this reading, MayTrip holds NO points, MomCess holds NO points. IEEE 2030.5's high-voltage ride-through curves are carried by SunSpec model 708 (DERTripHV), which is model 707's geometry point for point — the same three sub-curves, the same (V VNomPct, Tms Secs) pairs, the same Tms-is-seconds trap — applied to the over-voltage side AND this row published <opModHVRTMomentaryCessation> 2 breakpoint(s) (0, 10000) (1200, 10000) (x multiplier 10^-2, y multiplier 10^-2) = (0 s, 100) (12 s, 100) in the device's own units, yRefType=4 (%setEffectiveV) northbound and the DER's M708 DER Trip HV MomCess sub-curve adopt handshake never COMPLETED, so no curve-set was taken up: M708 DER Trip HV live curve-set MomCess — Ena=0 (disabled), adopt req=0 rslt=0 (not COMPLETED), NPt=8 NCrvSet=2 (each set holds MustTrip/MayTrip/MomCess), curve-set read-only=true, MustTrip s/%VNom points: (13, 110) (0.16, 120), MayTrip holds NO points, MomCess holds NO points <- this reading. IEEE 2030.5's high-voltage ride-through curves are carried by SunSpec model 708 (DERTripHV), which is model 707's geometry point for point — the same three sub-curves, the same (V VNomPct, Tms Secs) pairs, the same Tms-is-seconds trap — applied to the over-voltage side AUTHORED BUT NOT DEVICE-MAPPABLE: this row also placed <opModLVRTMustTrip> yRefType=4 (%setEffectiveV), <opModLVRTMomentaryCessation> yRefType=4 (%setEffectiveV), <opModHVRTMustTrip> yRefType=4 (%setEffectiveV), <opModHVRTMomentaryCessation> yRefType=4 (%setEffectiveV) on the wire, and no SunSpec ride-through bank on either generation carries a DeptRef register: 707/708 fix their y axis at VNomPct (per cent of NOMINAL voltage) and 709/710 at absolute Hz, so there is nothing on the device that could hold the yRefType this row served. 12. **PASS** — the legacy ride-through writer this product will call CANNOT disable a protective trip boundary as a failure action — structurally, not by convention
   - Method: this suite's own row construction (no observation involved) — lexa-proto/derbase/legacycurve.go's own documented behaviour, exercised from this package by TestLegacyRideThroughRefusesCaseBAndLeavesTheBoundaryUp, TestLegacyRideThroughIsNotReachableThroughTheFailClosedWriter and TestReleaseIsNoOpinionOnTheProtectiveModels
   - Observed: THIS IS A PROPERTY OF THE WRITER, NOT A MEASUREMENT OF THIS RUN (the criterion beside it carries that). Three facts, each from lexa-proto/derbase/legacycurve.go's own doc comments: (1) the fail-closed helper that writes ModEna=0 takes a nonProtectiveCurve WITNESS which only nonProtective() can mint and which refuses models 129 and 130, so "the ride-through writer cannot reach the fail-closed helper — not 'does not', CANNOT"; (2) WriteLegacyRideThrough REFUSES the Case-B single-bank rewrite with reason legacy-ride-through-single-bank, because "rewriting the live bank means deleting the trip boundary for the width of the transaction, and a protective setting is not something to momentarily delete"; (3) its failure arm RESTORES the ActCrv selection recorded at engage and never writes ModEna, "because a known-wrong trip boundary is better than an unknown one". A fourth, from ReleaseLegacyCurve: releasing a control on 129/130 is NoOpinion — "ride-through is a protective trip boundary and is never stripped by a release"

10. **PASS** — the legacy ride-through writer this product will call CANNOT disable a protective trip boundary as a failure action — structurally, not by convention
   - Method: this suite's own row construction (no observation involved) — lexa-proto/derbase/legacycurve.go's own documented behaviour, exercised from this package by TestLegacyRideThroughRefusesCaseBAndLeavesTheBoundaryUp, TestLegacyRideThroughIsNotReachableThroughTheFailClosedWriter and TestReleaseIsNoOpinionOnTheProtectiveModels
   - Observed: THIS IS A PROPERTY OF THE WRITER, NOT A MEASUREMENT OF THIS RUN (the criterion beside it carries that). Three facts, each from lexa-proto/derbase/legacycurve.go's own doc comments: (1) the fail-closed helper that writes ModEna=0 takes a nonProtectiveCurve WITNESS which only nonProtective() can mint and which refuses models 129 and 130, so "the ride-through writer cannot reach the fail-closed helper — not 'does not', CANNOT"; (2) WriteLegacyRideThrough REFUSES the Case-B single-bank rewrite with reason legacy-ride-through-single-bank, because "rewriting the live bank means deleting the trip boundary for the width of the transaction, and a protective setting is not something to momentarily delete"; (3) its failure arm RESTORES the ActCrv selection recorded at engage and never writes ModEna, "because a known-wrong trip boundary is better than an unknown one". A fourth, from ReleaseLegacyCurve: releasing a control on 129/130 is NoOpinion — "ride-through is a protective trip boundary and is never stripped by a release"

### ✗ csip-conf-v1.3::BASIC-007 Basic Inverter Control (Ramp Rates) [C, A, S] — FAIL

Reference: CSIP-CONF-v1.3 V1.3 §BASIC-007 - Basic Inverter Control (Ramp Rates) [C, A, S] (no numeric section number printed; test-ID heading only)

Live-phase observation (what the check's own socket saw; the numbered assertions below are re-derived from the capture and are the verdict): drove gridsim program 0's DefaultDERControl (CSIP CTP v1.3 Figure 3's Service Point program) first to a DISTINGUISHABLE setGradW=5000 baseline (WRmp=50 — a value Figure 7 does not name, commanded only so the move to the Test Value is observable from any prior register state), confirmed that landing in the DER's own registers, then commanded Figure 7's Test Values setGradW=9000/setSoftGradW=400 and waited 0s for the DUT's poll cycle. This row's own printed procedure is Default-Only: it schedules no DERControl event and requires no DERControlResponse; independent southbound oracle: FAIL — the DER's own model 704 WRmp register reads 0 against a commanded setGradW=9000 (90.00%); model 703 ESRmpTms reads 300 s. REPORTED, NOT ASSERTED: this row's own procedure is Default-Only and schedules no enter-service transition, and lexa-gw only writes ESRmpTms from setSoftGradW when one actually executes (cmd/modbus/reconcile_adv.go's resolveEnergizeRampS runs only when an energize axis is in force) — a commanded setSoftGradW=400 would resolve to 25.0 s if that transition were ever exercised, which this row does not do; poll-cycle window: 2m30s, derived from the bench 2030.5 server's advertised pollRate of 1m0s (poll_rate_s from http://192.168.0.188:11114/admin/status), which is the rate a client in poll_rate_mode "honor" — the product default — paces its walk at, the configured interval being only a floor beneath it — it is the slower of that and the DUT's own IEEE 2030.5 discovery interval floor of 1m0s (discovery_interval_s in /etc/lexa/northbound.json), and the slower governs: 2 periods plus 30s of slack, so a period that had just elapsed still leaves a whole one inside the window

M706 Volt-Watt — Ena=1 (ENABLED), adopt req=0 rslt=1 (COMPLETED), NPt=10 NCrv=2, live curve read-only=true, DeptRef=0 (W_MAX_PCT), RspTms=0 s (open-loop response), live curve V/W points: (100, 100) (105, 100) (109, 0)) — the curve's breakpoints MOVED, so the match is evidence this control was executed, not content an earlier run left in the register bank; the curve-bound control's mRID is the SERVER's (DERC-SP-CURVE-1788990919), not this row's synthetic CERT-BASIC-011-ed61bab1: gridsim mints it for a POST /admin/curve and ignores any mRID the request carries, so every wire criterion on this row binds the minted one; poll-cycle window: 2m30s, derived from the bench 2030.5 server's advertised pollRate of 1m0s (poll_rate_s from http://192.168.0.188:11114/admin/status), which is the rate a client in poll_rate_mode "honor" — the product default — paces its walk at, the configured interval being only a floor beneath it — it is the slower of that and the DUT's own IEEE 2030.5 discovery interval floor of 1m0s (discovery_interval_s in /etc/lexa/northbound.json), and the slower governs: 2 periods plus 30s of slack, so a period that had just elapsed still leaves a whole one inside the window; VERDICT RECONCILED — the live phase declared SKIP from what the socket saw; the citation phase re-derived the criteria from the capture and the case is PASS. ### ✗ csip-conf-v1.3::BASIC-012 Basic Inverter Control (Frequency Droop/Frequency-Watt) [C, A, S] — FAIL

Reference: CSIP-CONF-v1.3 V1.3 §BASIC-012 - Basic Inverter Control (Frequency Droop/Frequency-Watt) [C, A, S] (no numeric section number printed; test-ID heading only)

Live-phase observation (what the check's own socket saw; the numbered assertions below are re-derived from the capture and are the verdict): published a DERControl (CERT-BASIC-012-ed61bab1) carrying opModFreqWatt and waited 1m15s for the DUT to fetch it; independent southbound oracle: FAIL — this row also published 4 breakpoint(s) (5900, 100) (5950, 80) (6050, 80) (6200, 0) (x multiplier 10^-2, y multiplier 10^0) northbound, and this DER's generation stores frequency-watt BREAKPOINTS in no register at all (IEEE 2030.5's opModFreqWatt is a BREAKPOINT curve (frequency → watts), and the SunSpec/IEEE-1547 7xx set has no model that stores frequency-watt breakpoints: frequency response is expressed as model 711 (DER Frequency Droop), a PARAMETRIC control — deadbands DbOf/DbUf, gains KOf/KUf, a response time — with no point table a published curve could be written into. This product's own reconciler records the same conclusion for the same reason (lexa-gw cmd/modbus/reconcile_adv.go, on releasing its freq-watt axis: "No SunSpec model executes freq-watt ... nothing to disable on release either"), and its advAxisModel table has no model for the axis at all. So the BREAKPOINTS are authored northbound — that half is real evidence about what the DUT was offered — and nothing southbound on a 7xx DER can hold them. This is NOT closed by asserting something weaker against 711, which is a different function; it stays open, and it IS closed on the other generation, where model 134 stores frequency-watt breakpoints (see mappingFreqWattLegacy)) — so the curve half is served and NOT asserted here, and what follows is about the parametric droop only. this row published opModFreqDroop dBOF=60030 dBUF=59970 (thousandths of Hz) kOF=40 kUF=40 (thousandths, unitless) openLoopTms=600 (hundredths of a second) and the DER's M711 Frequency Droop adopt handshake never COMPLETED, so no droop was taken up: M711 Frequency Droop — Ena=0 (disabled), adopt req=0 rslt=0 (not COMPLETED), NPt=0 NCrv=2, live curve read-only=true, no breakpoint table (parametric control): DbOf=0.05 Hz DbUf=0.05 Hz KOf=20 KUf=20 RspTms=5 s PMin=0. IEEE 2030.5's opModFreqDroop is a PARAMETRIC frequency-droop control — two dead bands, two per-unit gains and an open-loop response time — and its exact register home is SunSpec model 711 (DER Frequency Droop), whose Ctl block carries precisely those five quantities: DbOf/DbUf (scaled by Db_SF), KOf/KUf (scaled by K_SF) and RspTms (scaled by RspTms_SF). The correspondence is EXACT and needs no interpretation: IEEE Std 2030.5-2018's FreqDroopType (p.242) states dBOF/dBUF in thousandths of hertz and kOF/kUF in thousandths unitless as 'per-unit frequency change ... corresponding to one per-unit power output change', and model 711's own documentation states the same quantity in the same words, so the translation is five fixed decimal shifts and no nominal-frequency assumption enters anywhere (see curveBinding.want711, which performs it independently from the standards' text rather than from the product's table) DELIVERY: the DUT FETCHED this row's control from the bench during its window (2 control-list GET(s), the last at 2026-09-09T17:57:56-04:00 on the server's clock), so it was live and the control reached it; the curve-bound control's mRID is the SERVER's (DERC-SP-CURVE-1788991019), not this row's synthetic CERT-BASIC-012-ed61bab1: gridsim mints it for a POST /admin/curve and ignores any mRID the request carries, so every wire criterion on this row binds the minted one; poll-cycle window: 2m30s, derived from the bench 2030.5 server's advertised pollRate of 1m0s (poll_rate_s from http://192.168.0.188:11114/admin/status), which is the rate a client in poll_rate_mode "honor" — the product default — paces its walk at, the configured interval being only a floor beneath it — it is the slower of that and the DUT's own IEEE 2030.5 discovery interval floor of 1m0s (discovery_interval_s in /etc/lexa/northbound.json), and the slower governs: 2 periods plus 30s of slack, so a period that had just elapsed still leaves a whole one inside the window

IEEE 2030.5's opModFreqDroop is a PARAMETRIC frequency-droop control — two dead bands, two per-unit gains and an open-loop response time — and its exact register home is SunSpec model 711 (DER Frequency Droop), whose Ctl block carries precisely those five quantities: DbOf/DbUf (scaled by Db_SF), KOf/KUf (scaled by K_SF) and RspTms (scaled by RspTms_SF). The correspondence is EXACT and needs no interpretation: IEEE Std 2030.5-2018's FreqDroopType (p.242) states dBOF/dBUF in thousandths of hertz and kOF/kUF in thousandths unitless as 'per-unit frequency change ... corresponding to one per-unit power output change', and model 711's own documentation states the same quantity in the same words, so the translation is five fixed decimal shifts and no nominal-frequency assumption enters anywhere (see curveBinding.want711, which performs it independently from the standards' text rather than from the product's table) DELIVERY: the DUT FETCHED this row's control from the bench during its window (2 control-list GET(s), the last at 2026-09-09T17:57:56-04:00 on the server's clock), so it was live and the control reached it

### ✓ csip-conf-v1.3::BASIC-015 BASIC-015 – Advanced Inverter Control [C, A, S] — PASS

Reference: CSIP-CONF-v1.3 V1.3 §BASIC test-case series (Advanced Inverter Control) — the test ID is the heading

Live-phase observation (what the check's own socket saw; the numbered assertions below are re-derived from the capture and are the verdict): published a DERControl (CERT-BASIC-015-ed61bab1) carrying opModWattPF and waited 24s for the DUT to fetch it; independent southbound oracle: PASS — nothing of the SunSpec model 712 (DER Watt-Var) curve bank moved in the DER's own registers across this row's window: they read M712 Watt-Var — Ena=0 (disabled), adopt req=0 rslt=0 (not COMPLETED), NPt=10 NCrv=2, live curve read-only=true, DeptRef=1 (VAR_MAX_PCT), live curve W/var points: (100, 5) (200, -5) | M712 Watt-Var staging curve 1 — read-only=false, DeptRef=0 (W_MAX_PCT), this curve holds NO points both before this row published its control and after the DUT's poll cycle, so the refusal left no southbound trace. opModWattPF's only exact register home is LEGACY SunSpec model 131 (Watt-PF). The 7xx set has no watt-PF model at all: model 712 is DER Watt-Var, whose y axis means a signed percentage of a var reference (its DeptRef names %VArMax or %VArAval) rather than a signed power-factor displacement under the EEI convention, so writing a PF curve into it commands a different function. IEEE Std 2030.5-2018 says the same thing from the other side: opModWattVar and opModWattPF are separate DERControlBase elements (p.251) with separate DERCurveType codes, 14 and 13 (p.254). This sentence cited the pre-publication draft and its codes (10 and 2) until IW15-030; the argument was always right and its authority was the wrong document, which on a string that lands in certification bundles is a defect of its own. On a 7xx DER, therefore, the honest answer is cannot-comply at receipt — which is what this row measures THERE, instead of grading a PF curve against a var bank and calling the substitution a PASS. On a LEGACY 12x DER the same row is an EXECUTION row against model 131, because that DER does have the axis; see mappingWattPFLegacy. The two halves are different KINDS of assertion, not one assertion against two banks, and which applies is decided by the device the bench is running DELIVERY: the DUT FETCHED this row's control from the bench during its window (2 control-list GET(s), the last at 2026-09-09T18:07:00-04:00 on the server's clock), so it was live and the control reached it; the curve-bound control's mRID is the SERVER's (DERC-SP-CURVE-1788991599), not this row's synthetic CERT-BASIC-015-ed61bab1: gridsim mints it for a POST /admin/curve and ignores any mRID the request carries, so every wire criterion on this row binds the minted one; poll-cycle window: 2m30s, derived from the bench 2030.5 server's advertised pollRate of 1m0s (poll_rate_s from http://192.168.0.188:11114/admin/status), which is the rate a client in poll_rate_mode "honor" — the product default — paces its walk at, the configured interval being only a floor beneath it — it is the slower of that and the DUT's own IEEE 2030.5 discovery interval floor of 1m0s (discovery_interval_s in /etc/lexa/northbound.json), and the slower governs: 2 periods plus 30s of slack, so a period that had just elapsed still leaves a whole one inside the window; VERDICT RECONCILED — the live phase declared SKIP from what the socket saw; the citation phase re-derived the criteria from the capture and the case is PASS. The capture-derived verdict is the one that stands: it is the only one a reader of this bundle can repeat.

### ✓ local-ext-v1::EXT-001 EXT-001 - opModWattVar curve execution against SunSpec model 712 — PASS

**LOCAL EXTENSION — certifiable under no standard.** No published procedure covers this case: its Test Values are the harness's own and its verdict is supplementary PRODUCT EVIDENCE, not a conformance result. It is run, bundled and re-verified like any other row, and it is excluded from every applicable-FAIL tally and from the clean-run criterion. A FAIL here is a finding about the implementation; it is not a certification failure.

Reference: LOCAL-EXT-v1 v1 (local extension family; not a published specification) §1.1 EXT-001 - opModWattVar curve execution (under 1 Local product-evidence extensions)

Live-phase observation (what the check's own socket saw; the numbered assertions below are re-derived from the capture and are the verdict): published a DERControl (CERT-EXT-001-ed61bab1) carrying opModWattVar and waited 2m1s for the DUT to fetch it; independent southbound oracle: PASS — the DER did NOT hold this row's content before it was published (this row published 3 breakpoint(s) (0, 0) (5000, 0) (10000, -3000) (x multiplier 10^-2, y multiplier 10^-2) northbound and the DER's M712 Watt-Var adopt handshake never COMPLETED, so no curve was taken up: M712 Watt-Var — Ena=0 (disabled), adopt req=0 rslt=0 (not COMPLETED), NPt=10 NCrv=2, live curve read-only=true, DeptRef=1 (VAR_MAX_PCT), live curve W/var points: (100, 5) (200, -5). IEEE 2030.5's opModWattVar is the Q(P) function, whose SunSpec/IEEE-1547 carriage is model 712 (DER Watt-Var) — the correspondence this product's southbound reconciler uses, and the one BASIC-015 proves NEGATIVELY by requiring that opModWattPF never reach it) and DOES hold it after the DUT's poll cycle (the DER's own M712 Watt-Var live curve holds exactly the breakpoints this row published, its adopt handshake COMPLETED and the function is enabled: the device's live curve holds exactly the published breakpoints, in order: (0, 0) (50, 0) (100, -30). M712 Watt-Var — Ena=1 (ENABLED), adopt req=0 rslt=1 (COMPLETED), NPt=10 NCrv=2, live curve read-only=true, DeptRef=2 (VAR_AVAL_PCT), live curve W/var points: (0, 0) (50, 0) (100, -30)) — the curve's breakpoints MOVED, so the match is evidence this control was executed, not content an earlier run left in the register bank; the curve-bound control's mRID is the SERVER's (DERC-SP-CURVE-1788993257), not this row's synthetic CERT-EXT-001-ed61bab1: gridsim mints it for a POST /admin/curve and ignores any mRID the request carries, so every wire criterion on this row binds the minted one; poll-cycle window: 2m30s, derived from the bench 2030.5 server's advertised pollRate of 1m0s (poll_rate_s from http://192.168.0.188:11114/admin/status), which is the rate a client in poll_rate_mode "honor" — the product default — paces its walk at, the configured interval being only a floor beneath it — it is the slower of that and the DUT's own IEEE 2030.5 discovery interval floor of 1m0s (discovery_interval_s in /etc/lexa/northbound.json), and the slower governs: 2 periods plus 30s of slack, so a period that had just elapsed still leaves a whole one inside the window; VERDICT RECONCILED — the live phase declared SKIP from what the socket saw; the citation phase re-derived the criteria from the capture and the case is PASS. The capture-derived verdict is the one that stands: it is the only one a reader of this bundle can repeat.
